A DAG revision that assumed a later step -- the FBI `derived` confidence class -- wrote values its own check constraint refused, against a warehouse that had not received the step |

### ETL and Shared Unit Tests

All tests in this section use local fixtures and mocked boundaries.

| ID | Priority | Type / markers | Test | Pass metric | Failure signal |
|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| ETL-001 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Census geography parameters | `us`, two-digit state, and three-digit county inputs produce the exact Census API `for`/`in` parameters | Incorrect parameter or invalid FIPS accepted |
| ETL-002 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Census canonical geography IDs | US maps to `us:1`; state maps to `state:SS`; county maps to `state:SS\|county:CCC` | Wrong ID or loss of zero padding |
| ETL-003 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Census invalid geography | Missing/malformed FIPS and unsupported levels return `None` or raise the documented validation error | Invalid geography becomes a usable key |
| ETL-004 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Census response conversion | A representative header/row fixture produces the expected row count, variable codes, estimate/MOE roles, geographies, and numeric values | Field shift, row loss, or wrong type |
| ETL-005 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Census malformed/empty response | Empty data raises `CensusNoContent`; malformed headers/rows raise a deterministic validation error | Silent success, index error, or partial corrupt frame |
| ETL-006 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Census sentinel/null values | Documented source sentinels and blanks become null; valid negative values remain numeric | Sentinel stored as fact or valid value discarded |
| ETL-007 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | ACS duration | ACS1 spans one calendar year; ACS5 spans estimate year minus four through estimate year | Any start/end date differs |
| ETL-008 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Census chunk boundaries | Sizes 0, 1, `n`, `n+1`, and exact multiples preserve order and every item exactly once | Missing, duplicated, reordered, or oversized chunk |
| ETL-009 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | BLS LAUS parser and builder | Published 20-character state/county series round-trip with exact program, measure, FIPS, and `geo_id` | Round-trip mismatch or invalid length accepted |
| ETL-010 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | BLS unsupported geography | National LAUS, metro/city, bad padding, and malformed IDs are rejected | Unsupported geography receives a canonical ID |
| ETL-011 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | BLS response parsing | Representative monthly records produce expected series, period, value, footnotes, and source metadata | Row count or normalized field differs |
| ETL-012 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | BLS empty/error response | Empty results, API error status, and daily-threshold response map to the documented exception classes | Error treated as successful empty data or wrong retry class |
| ETL-013 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | BLS period normalization | M01-M12, Q01-Q04, S01-S02, and A01 return exact period and duration dates including leap-year February | Any boundary date differs |
| ETL-014 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | BLS chunk boundaries | Sizes around the configured API batch limit preserve every series exactly once and in order | Lost, duplicated, reordered, or oversized request |
| ETL-015 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | FRED domain ownership | Every curated series belongs to exactly one configured domain and configured order is stable | Missing, extra, or multiply owned series |
| ETL-016 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | FRED response parsing | Representative observations produce exact date/value/series fields and deterministic row count | Field mismatch or unexpected row loss |
| ETL-017 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | FRED missing/malformed observations | `"."`, blank, null, invalid JSON, and truncated entries follow the documented null/error behavior | Invalid numeric fact stored or uncontrolled exception |
| ETL-018 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | FRED duration normalization | Daily, weekly, biweekly, monthly, quarterly, semiannual, and annual inputs return exact inclusive date ranges | Boundary or frequency mapping differs |
| ETL-019 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | FRED chunk boundaries | Sizes around the configured series batch limit preserve every series exactly once | Missing, duplicate, or oversized batch |
| ETL-020 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | HTTP retry classification | 429, 500, 502, 503, timeout, and network failure are retryable; validation and other 4xx errors are not | Wrong retry class or unbounded retry |
| ETL-021 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Retry budget | Mocked retryable failures stop after the configured attempt count and expose the final cause | Too many/few attempts or swallowed cause |
| ETL-022 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Numeric parsing limits | Valid integers/decimals, negatives, high precision, nulls, and overflow inputs have explicit outcomes | Precision loss, overflow acceptance, or crash |
| ETL-023 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Duplicate-record normalization | Duplicate natural keys reduce according to the declared latest/source rule with one deterministic survivor | More than one survivor or unstable choice |
| ETL-024 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Transform dimension mapping | Matching time/geography rows receive correct surrogate keys; missing matches increment metrics and follow the declared retain/drop rule | Wrong key, silent loss, or wrong miss count |
| ETL-025 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Transform metrics | Input, output, null, dimension-hit/miss, and inserted counts reconcile: input equals categorized outcomes | Counts do not reconcile or negative metric |
| ETL-026 | P1 | Unit / `unit` | Source hash/change detection | Reordered identical inputs produce the same hash; changed membership/value produces a different hash | Nondeterministic hash or missed change |
| ETL-027 | P1 | Unit / `unit` | Gold shard construction | Requested date range produces complete, non-overlapping, ordered shard boundaries | Gap, overlap, out-of-range shard, or empty required shard |
| ETL-028 | P1 | Unit / `unit` | Gold DDL hash behavior | Identical ordered DDL has a stable hash; content change changes the hash | Hash drift or unchanged hash after content change |
| ETL-029 | P1 | Unit / `unit` | Data-quality checks | Valid source fixtures return zero violations; one deliberately bad row returns the exact expected violation count | False positive, false negative, or wrong source routing |
| ETL-030 | P1 | Unit / `unit` | Configuration validation | Valid defaults load; missing connection ID, empty configured scope, duplicate ownership, and invalid batch sizes raise clear errors | Invalid configuration accepted or secret echoed |
| ETL-031 | P1 | Unit / `unit` | ACS dataset-name handling | Unknown and empty names use the documented one-year fallback; ACS1 and ACS5 names are case-insensitive | Unexpected duration, crash, or case-sensitive known dataset |
| ETL-032 | P1 | Unit / `unit` | BLS metadata query routing | State and county metadata queries use exact supported prefixes, retain requested geography, and reject unsupported scopes before database work | Wrong prefix/filter, cross-geography series, or invalid scope reaches database |
| ETL-033 | P1 | Unit / `unit` | BLS national-series routing | National LAUS requests are rejected and curated national labor measures remain routed through their published CPS/LN series | Synthetic national LAUS key accepted or required CPS series removed |
| ETL-034 | P1 | Unit / `unit` | Curated-series recommendation contract | Required recommended BLS and FRED platform series remain present in the curated configuration | Required recommended series disappears from configuration |
| ETL-035 | P1 | Unit / `unit` | BLS unknown-period fallback | Empty, null, and unrecognized BLS period codes follow the documented annual fallback | Unknown period crashes or produces a non-annual duration |
| ETL-036 | P1 | Unit / `unit` | FRED unknown-frequency fallback | Null and empty FRED frequency values follow the documented daily fallback | Unknown frequency crashes or produces a non-daily duration |
| ETL-037 | P1 | Contract + database / `unit integration database` | Incremental serving implementation contract | Source refreshes are watermarked and affected-key scoped; checkpoints, annual changed-history chunks, progress logs, and unchanged-row watermark preservation remain wired; ACS latest selection analyzes affected keys and uses bounded per-key lookups while selecting across complete history | A source returns to full rebuild behavior, loses checkpoints/progress, rewrites unchanged watermarks, or ACS latest selection returns to a global historical-row sort |
| ETL-038 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Request fingerprint safety | Canonical source/endpoint/parameters produce a stable fingerprint and secret-bearing fields are rejected | Equivalent requests drift or credentials enter fingerprint inputs |
| ETL-039 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Dedicated capture commit | Payload plus envelope commit on their own connection; envelope failure rolls back both and closes the connection | Parser transaction can own capture commit or partial capture persists |
| ETL-040 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | Offline capture loading | Replay returns stored bytes only after checksum verification and performs no network work | Missing checksum verification, changed bytes, or network dependency |
| ETL-041 | P1 | Unit / `unit` | Gold bootstrap detection | Required-object detection renders valid SQL for every combination of required relations and procedures, including an empty procedure list; a component with no required objects is vacuously bootstrapped | A trailing comma or empty predicate list produces invalid SQL, or bootstrap state is misdetected |
| ETL-042 | P0 | Contract + database / `unit integration database` | FBI agency aggregation boundary | Gold products retain national/state provider totals and agency observations at their published grain; county/place views only filter effective-dated agency relationships, never aggregate observation values or publish area-total labels | A provider total is reconstructed, an agency value is aggregated or mislabeled as an area total, or a valid historical observation loses its effective area filter after a later refresh |
| ETL-043 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | PEP release contract across decades | A release states its own observation range; the vintage equality holds only for a postcensal series, so an intercensal file closing an earlier decade can be registered; a partitioned or archived product declares the files it is captured from; the 2020s releases are unchanged | A closed decade that cannot be registered, a range ending after its own vintage accepted, a partition set that cannot address its files, or a current-decade release whose contract moved |
| ETL-044 | P0 | Contract + database / `unit integration database` | PEP seam resolution across overlapping products | The latest relation partitions on measure, geography and year rather than on the publishing product, and ranks intercensal before postcensal, then newest vintage, then the product publishing that geography in its own right; exactly one row survives and the revision surface still shows every publication | A geography answered twice for one period, a seam year answered once per decade, a tie left to the planner, or a revision hidden by the choice |
| ETL-045 | P0 | Unit / `unit` | PEP wide-CSV parser across decades | Required columns and completeness thresholds come from the registry, so a newly registered product can be complete; the pre-2020 NATURALINC spelling maps onto the canonical measure; the decennial count column is read as its own measure dated to April; a published not-applicable mark is a sentinel | A decade split into two measures that meet at 2020, a census count published as a July estimate, a product incomplete by construction, or a documented non-value reported as a parse failure |
| ETL-046 | P0 | Unit + database / `unit integration database` | PEP pre-CSV readers | The printed intercensal tables are read from each block's own header rather than fixed offsets, an area name that wrapped keeps its name and its values, and the 1990s cell file's total is summed from the published cells with the source text retained and the derivation declared; one county's series spans 1971 to 2025 with the published gap left as a gap | A shifted column read as a value, a wrapped row lost or orphaned, a derived total presented as printed, or an absent year interpolated |
| ETL-047 | P0 | Contract + database / `unit integration database` | Served geography vocabulary | Every reporting refresh writes the normalised `NATIONAL`/`STATE`/`COUNTY` vocabulary, never `silver_ref.dim_geo`'s own `us`/`state`/`county` spelling; the fact views hold the single definition of that mapping, and a refresh run with a `us:1` dimension row present still serves `NATIONAL` | A served relation carries `us`, so `geo_level=NATIONAL` answers zero rows through the dispatch's `UPPER(geo_level) = UPPER(:geo_level)` filter while the unfiltered read answers the row |
| ETL-048 | P0 | Contract + database / `unit integration database` | BLS LAUS measure identity | The seven LAUS measures are the BLS catalog identity for program `LA`, so one metric spans every published state and county; every other program keeps its series identity; the serving refresh branches on the measure dimension and keeps `series_id` on every row; the publisher emits one row per measure with grains aggregated from the fact rows and excludes the measure-identified program's series | LAUS returns to one metric per place (no BLS metric spans geographies, so the map, bins, and comparison have nothing to draw), a program is published twice, grains are declared as a constant, or lineage back to the BLS series is lost |
| ETL-049 | P0 | Contract + database / `unit integration database` | Forced full serving re-serve | Every union-served source declares an all-years plan carrying no watermark predicate and no bound parameters, spanning its reporting relation as well as silver; a forced run uses that plan and rewrites years the changed-year plan skips; it is never the default and no scheduled run selects it; a source declaring no such plan refuses rather than silently falling back; a forced run resumes an interrupted one under the same durable run marker and skips only the years that run already finished; repeating a completed forced re-serve changes no row counts; and a forced run never advances the source watermark past the genuine silver watermark | A meaning change left half-applied because the changed-year plan skipped the years still carrying the old meaning, a multi-hour re-serve restarting from the first year after a retry, an expensive re-serve running on a schedule, or a re-serve advancing the watermark so a later ingest silently skips rows |
| ETL-050 | P0 | Contract + database / `unit fbi_ucr`, `integration database` | A county resolved from a provider label says so | A county established by matching the FBI's county label to the boundary reference is published as `resolution_method = 'county_label_match'`, `confidence_class = 'derived'` -- never the `reviewed` the place path earns from its crosswalk -- and DQ-FBI-004 fails any resolved relationship claiming a confidence its method did not earn, including a method the reviewed mapping does not know. A label that matched nothing carries `county_label_unmatched` rather than asserting the county is absent, and the agency carries `agency_county_unresolved` rather than `agency_only`. The operations guide says how to review one and why the comparison is not relaxed | An area filter publishing `reviewed` over a name match, so a consumer filtering on that token cannot tell a reviewed mapping from a name join; a zero-match join asserting a county is absent when the label simply does not normalise; or a provider-labelled county that failed to resolve reading as an agency the provider never associated with a county |
| ETL-051 | P1 | Contract / `unit` | A zero-instance expansion does not skip the work behind it | Every task a dynamically mapped task gates, directly or transitively, runs on `none_failed` rather than Airflow's default `all_success`: Airflow marks a mapped task that expands to zero instances *skipped*, and `all_success` reads that skip as reason not to run while `none_failed` still refuses to run behind an upstream that failed. The wiring is read statically from `dags/*.py` -- task functions, `>>` chains, and the implicit dependency an argument creates -- and any name the reader cannot resolve to a task fails the audit rather than going unchecked, with the three expanding provider DAGs and their gated work both asserted so the contract cannot pass vacuously | `bls_ingest_dag` declared `all_success` on all seven tasks from `ensure_silver_schema` to `emit_bls_publisher_ready`, where ACS and FRED declared `none_failed`. Nothing said which was intended and no test read the rule at all. BLS's plan builder always includes the rolling window, so the plan is not empty today and the divergence cost nothing yet -- but a plan that ever came back empty would have skipped the silver transform, the gold refresh, the chunked serving refresh and the publisher-ready event, serving nothing for a run whose only news was that there was nothing new to fetch, and reporting success for it |

### PostgreSQL Integration Tests

PostgreSQL integration tests apply repository DDL to clean isolated state in the pinned PostGIS 16 container. They never point at a developer's default database unless an explicit test-only opt-in is set.

| ID | Priority | Type / markers | Test | Pass metric | Failure signal |
|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| DB-001 | P1 | Integration / `integration database` | Clean bootstrap | All reference, raw, silver, gold, and contract DDL applies in documented order with exit 0 | Any missing dependency or SQL error |
| DB-002 | P1 | Integration / `integration database` | Intended DDL rerun | A second application succeeds and object counts/definitions remain stable | Duplicate-object error or unintended object change |
| DB-003 | P1 | Integration / `integration database` | Primary/unique constraints | One representative duplicate natural key per source is rejected or upserted as declared | Duplicate fact or ledger row persists |
| DB-004 | P1 | Integration / `integration database` | Foreign-key integrity | Invalid dimension references are rejected; valid references insert successfully | Orphan accepted or valid row rejected |
| DB-005 | P1 | Integration / `integration database` | Check constraints | Invalid status, year/date range, measure type, and negative row count are rejected | Any invalid row commits |
| DB-006 | P1 | Integration / `integration database` | Raw ingestion idempotency | Replaying each source fixture leaves the same natural-key count and zero duplicates | Count increases or duplicate key exists |
| DB-007 | P1 | Integration / `integration database` | Changed-slice handling | Changed hash marks/replaces only the intended stale slice and loads the revised value once | Unrelated slice changes, stale value served, or duplicate revision |
| DB-008 | P1 | Integration / `integration database` | Census revision-to-silver | Expected fixture natural keys, values, time keys, and geography keys match exactly | Missing/extra row or value/key mismatch |
| DB-009 | P1 | Integration / `integration database` | BLS revision-to-silver | Expected fixture natural keys, values, periods, time keys, and geography keys match exactly | Missing/extra row or value/key mismatch |

The unit tier pins the relation, the property list and the agreement between `martin.yml`, both compose files, the proxy and the READMEs; the disposable stack's healthcheck gates on the same relation, so a tile layer that cannot be read stops the stack rather than serving empty tiles | A layer named `counties` serving 32,629 places the map refuses to draw. `tileGrains.ts` already declared STATE and COUNTY as the drawable grains and named PLACE as one the boundary cannot draw, so every place polygon was serialised, sent, decoded and hidden by the client's own filter -- measured at 90% of a 2 MB tile at zoom 4 |
| DB-054 | P0 | Unit / `unit shared`, Integration / `integration database` | Every source owns its relation DDL under `src/` | Each of the seven packages that publishes a `gold_*` subpackage is required to have at least one manifest asset under its own `DDL/` and one under its `gold_*/DDL/`; both halves, because a source owning only silver has gold defined somewhere else. CDC, FBI UCR and USDA NASS moved their control, silver, gold and publisher definitions out of `sql/migrations/010`-`012`, `014`, `020` and `022` and into their packages, and the rebuilt schema is byte-identical to the checked-in snapshot, which is what makes it a move rather than a rewrite. The superseded steps stay on disk as pointers at their new home and the manifest no longer applies them. The smoke tier's seed is checked to sort after every generated DDL prefix | `BETA_RESET_REINGESTION.md` said "the runtime DDL used by DAG tasks is packaged below `src/`", and for three of seven sources it was not. `022`'s own note recorded the consequence: FBI's publisher was "the one publisher whose last definition sits in a migration rather than a phase file, and that step is not edited in place", so correcting it needed a new migration each time, and `gold_cdc.metric_publisher` had accumulated three definitions across three files |
| DB-055 | P0 | Unit / `unit shared`, Integration / `integration database` | Every source's fact names its capture and says why a value is absent | All seven silver facts are checked against their own vocabulary -- FBI says `reported`/`not_reported`, ACS's number is `estimate_value` -- and the list is compared against the packages that publish gold, so a source added without an entry cannot be silently exempt. Each must reach `raw_capture.response_capture` directly or through its own revision relation, carry the provider's token, and constrain the published status to a non-null number. Census PEP is the one exclusion and it is stated: its fact declares `value NUMERIC NOT NULL`, so it has nothing to withhold | ACS and BLS carried none of it. Their revision relations distinguish absent/blank/sentinel/invalid and valid/missing/invalid; the fact aggregation kept only the number, so a cell Census suppressed reached serving as no row at all and a consumer could not tell it from one the Bureau never published. The guard also found a third: `silver_fred.fact_economic_indicators` had `value_status` with no constraint tying it to the value and a DEFAULT of `valid`, so a writer that set no status at all produced a row asserting a published value it did not have |
| DB-056 | P1 | Unit / `unit shared`, Integration / `integration database` | The ACS serving table is partitioned by year, and a chunk truncates a partition | `rpt_acs_observations` is `PARTITION BY RANGE (observation_date)` over a fixed 2000-2035 range, and the year refresh truncates the year's partition rather than deleting its date range. Two forced chunks over a scratch partition leave `n_dead_tup` at zero, and the measurement is guarded twice: the procedure must report `cleared_partitions=1`, so a chunk that silently took the delete path fails rather than measuring an untouched partition, and a companion test deletes from the same partition to prove the statistic moves in this database at all. Every served vintage must have its own partition; the default holds only the repository's 2099 fixture marker. Proven by forcing the delete path in the DDL and watching the guard fail with `cleared_partitions=0 deleted_rows=400` | A 45 GB heap with 48 GB of indexes, refreshed a year at a time by `DELETE ... WHERE observation_date BETWEEN` and re-insert. `BETA_RESET_REINGESTION.md` section 7 measured tens of millions of dead rows per chunk and a heap that grew as it was re-served, and its operator rule 2 read "vacuum manually; do not wait for autovacuum" -- a standing instruction to a person, for work the shape of the relation could do itself |
| DB-057 | P0 | Integration / `integration database` | A step that widens a vocabulary rewrites its rows before it constrains them | `025_county_label_is_not_reviewed.sql` is applied to a warehouse holding a row with the superseded `reviewed_county_name_crosswalk`, which is the one state it exists to correct, and must succeed and rewrite it to `county_label_match` / `derived`. Applying it twice is also asserted, because the manifest re-applies every asset on every bootstrap. Proven by restoring the shipped order and watching the guard fail with the constraint violation verbatim | The step shipped with both `ADD CONSTRAINT`s before the `UPDATE` that makes the rows satisfy them. `ADD CONSTRAINT` validates immediately, so it could only ever run against an empty table -- which is exactly what a fresh bootstrap gives it, so nothing reported the defect. Applying the manifest to the internal stack failed on it, and the message named the constraint rather than the ordering. Rewriting first is not sufficient either: the *old* constraint does not admit the new token, so both have to be dropped before the rewrite and added after it |
| DB-058 | P1 | Integration / `integration database` | What the refresh procedures say reaches the run's log | `_forward_procedure_notices` turns `connection.notices` into log records after each chunk's `CALL`: notices at info, `WARNING` at warning -- the refresh raises exactly one, that the relation is not partitioned, and it is the line in a twenty-chunk run that most needs not to look like progress. The list is cleared per chunk, asserted directly, because `notices` accumulates for the life of the connection and chunk twenty would otherwise re-log all twenty under the year it is currently serving | The per-chunk row counts and the `cleared_partitions=` marker that says whether a chunk truncated its partition (DB-056) went into a list nothing read. Found by writing the operator instruction "each chunk logs `cleared_partitions=1`" into the reset procedure, then running a real re-serve and grepping its Airflow log for it: zero occurrences. The instruction was unfollowable and the signal had never been visible to anyone |
| DB-059 | P0 | Integration / `integration database` | A value the provider withheld reaches the fact as withheld | A BLS observation carrying `'-'` and `value_status = 'missing'` transforms into a fact row with a null value, the status intact, the provider's own token beside it, and a `capture_id` resolving to the response it was read from. The refusal is exercised on a row that is otherwise accepted -- the same insert with a value succeeds first -- so the `CHECK` is shown to be about the status and the null together rather than about anything else in the row | The fact aggregation kept only the number, so a cell the provider suppressed and one it never published were the same row. 31,481,530 of 99,783,997 ACS fact rows on the internal stack carry no estimate, and every one was indistinguishable from a geography that does not exist |
| DB-060 | P1 | Integration / `integration database` | The latest-value lookup stops at a partition instead of asking all of them | `refresh_mv_acs_latest` walks partitions newest-first and exits when every key is resolved. Three tests: the superseded shape is *planned* to show it does scan every partition -- so the defect is demonstrated rather than described, and the test fails if PostgreSQL ever learns to prune it -- the per-partition step is planned to read exactly the one relation it was given with no `Merge Append`, and a key present only in 2003/2004/2006 still resolves to 2006, which is what stopping early could have broken. Duration is not asserted; a timing test on shared hardware measures the hardware | Partitioning the relation made this refresh three times slower: the newest row per key across all history is the one question time-partitioning is worst at, because the answer can be in any partition. 126 buffer hits to find one key's latest row against a single index scan before, and 1,294 seconds for a year's 4.4 million keys. Narrowed to 410, which makes the whole chunk faster than it was before the table was ever partitioned |

Mocked API tests are P0. Rows explicitly marked `integration` use disposable services.

There was no `robots` or `sitemap` either, so the reader's own workspace was not marked unpublished |
| WEB-109 | P0 | Unit / `frontend` | The transport boundary's literals and types are graded against the reviewed snapshot | Every resource this client addresses -- an argument to a transport function, or a `resource` an access-shape module hands back -- is a `GET` operation `tests/fixtures/api/openapi_contract.json` declares, with a templated one standing for every served path of its shape. Every property of a `types.ts` interface whose name the snapshot publishes as a schema is a property of that schema; an interface the snapshot does not name is this client's own view model and is not graded. The sweep asserts a floor on what it found, because a sweep that matches nothing passes silently | The client is hand-written on purpose -- a generated one loses the classified `ApiError` and the bounded paging -- and the cost is that a renamed field or a moved route is found by a reader. `servedContract.js` graded the test *fixtures* and nothing graded the application. `getFreshness` returned an untyped promise while the snapshot declared `FreshnessListResponse`, so `DataQualityExplorer` bypassed it with its own literal and its own type argument; four wrappers for the MVP-shaped observation routes had no caller at all, a second way to address a route that the handoff's "single transport boundary" forbids |
| WEB-110 | P0 | Unit / `frontend`, Browser / `frontend` | The table alternative reaches every loaded row, and says which | The explorer's observation table and the comparison's aligned table page the whole loaded set at 50 a page, with a caption naming the total, the range and the resource's declared order, and a keyboard-reachable Previous/Next. The page travels in the link as `rows=`, one-based, and survives a reload; a page past the end is the last page rather than an empty table, and a new selection returns to the first page while the *first* selection keeps the page the link named. The caption counts in the singular for one row. The accessibility suite's table-alternative assertion requires the caption | The explorer rendered `observations.slice(0, 12)` under a heading with no caption, no count and no control, and the comparison table did the same at 25. A national county map colours 3,144 geographies, and the README promises every value the map shows "remains available in the observation table" -- so a reader who cannot use the map reached twelve of them, with nothing on screen saying there were more. WEB-036, WEB-056 and WEB-059 made the bounded *read* honest; the bounded *render* said nothing at all |
| WEB-111 | P0 | Browser / `frontend` | An auditor runs on every route, and the route list is read from the application | `@axe-core/playwright` runs at WCAG 2.1 AA on every route derived from the `apps/web/app` directory -- the three retired redirect routes excluded, since auditing them audits their destination twice under the wrong path -- and passes with zero findings outside an allowlist of `(rule, selector, reason)` triples. Removing a button's accessible name fails it naming the route. `/workbench` joined the hand-written landmark and heading audit, and the control-name check covers every core route and reaches buttons and links, not only form controls. The active navigation link carries `aria-current="page"` and a skip link is the first focusable element on every page, landing focus on the content | WEB-025 promised "every core workflow" was audited against a nine-route list that `analytics-workbench` added `/workbench` to the application but not to, and `workbench.spec.js` checks none of the landmark, heading or status-row rules. No auditor ran at all: `@axe-core/playwright` was not a dependency, and hand-written assertions cannot see a contrast failure, a duplicate id, a button with no accessible name, or a table without headers. The auditor's first run found one: `/quality`'s evidence table scrolls and had no keyboard access to scroll it |
| WEB-112 | P0 | Unit / `frontend`, Browser / `frontend` | A provider's fact is published by the API, or it is not stated | A swept assertion over `apps/web/{app,components,lib}` fails on each claim this client is not entitled to make: a Census population threshold, a display-name override keyed on one Census variable, a hard-coded `census.gov` reference link, a hard-coded featured metric, and a client-authored coverage label for a dataset. `SourceNote` renders the source's own `reference_url` from `/catalog/sources` and no link where the source publishes none; `displayMetricName` answers the catalog's `metric_display_name` or the absence of one; the dataset selector shows the code the catalog answers; the home page's feature is the catalog's first answer, falling back to the catalog itself rather than to a metric nobody published | `AGENTS.md` forbids duplicating warehouse or API rules in client code and the frontend handoff forbids "a client-authored definition that could be mistaken for a provider fact". Four had accumulated. The coverage sentence stated a Census publication rule the API publishes no field for, so republishing the catalog could not correct it. The ACS guidance link rendered under *every* source, pointing a reader of BLS unemployment at the Census Bureau's guidance for a survey they were not reading. The display-name override made the one measure a reader is most likely to open the one measure whose label did not come from its source. The home page's featured link named a metric code even when the catalog had never published it |
| WEB-113 | P0 | Unit / `frontend`, Browser / `frontend`, Static | The map's bytes arrive when the map does | MapLibre is reached through `import()` inside `useMapLibre` and the comparison map through `next/dynamic` with `ssr: false`, so neither `/explore` nor `/compare` carries the library in its first load: the measured budgets fall from 1453 kB and 1471 kB to 528 kB and 550 kB, in line with every other route, and `check:bundle` fails on a return to a static import. In the browser, a selection the tile boundary cannot draw fetches no further JavaScript at all, and mounting the map fetches more than 300 kB that the first load did not carry -- compared as sets of chunks rather than by name, because a production build hashes its filenames. Each map surface states a failed chunk fetch and points at the table that carries the same values | Two routes costing four times every other one, paid by every reader including one whose selection has no map and one who never opens the map tab; or a silent return to a static import, which no test but a measured budget can see |
| WEB-114 | P0 | Unit / `frontend`, Browser / `frontend` | The browser can report what went wrong | Three reporters -- Web Vitals, `error`/`unhandledrejection`, and `securitypolicyviolation` -- build reports through one closed shape in `lib/clientReport` and post them to a same-origin Next route handler that writes one structured line to stdout and answers `204` to everything, including a report it refuses. The shape refuses a field outside it rather than dropping one: a token, a query string, an identifier or a measurement on a kind that measures nothing each make the whole report `null`, asserted field by field. A route is a path, stripped of its query by the builder and again by the sink, which does not trust the reporters. A message is bounded and has its control characters removed, so one report cannot be forged into two log lines. In the browser, a synthetic uncaught error and a synthetic `img-src` violation each reach the sink with the closed shape on the wire, no report carries the page's query string, and the policy names the sink through both `report-uri` and `report-to` without widening `connect-src` | "The frontend cannot be debugged" -- a CSP violation in production, a chunk blocked under the nonce policy, a WebGL failure or a hydration mismatch produced no signal anywhere, and the CSP browser spec proves zero violations only against a build CI made seconds earlier. Or the opposite failure: a diagnostic channel that quietly carries what a reader was looking at |

### Frontend Tests

| ID | Priority | Type / markers | Test | Pass metric | Failure signal |
|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| WEB-001 | P0 | Unit / `frontend` | Formatting and saved charts | Metric labels, empty values, replacement, cap, and corrupt local storage have exact deterministic outcomes | Display drift, lost replacement, uncapped storage, or crash |
| WEB-002 | P0 | Unit / `frontend` | Explorer view models | Metric/dataset choice, geography grain, observation join, pinned filter, distribution bins, legend counts, and no-data colors reconcile exactly, and a value the source did not publish (`null` or empty, which JavaScript coerces to a finite `0`) is excluded from every numeric path — choropleth colour, extrusion height, and formatted label — rather than becoming a published zero, while a genuine published `0` still renders; and the view-level control offers only the geography grains the selected measure declares in `valid_geo_grains`, attributing the narrowed list to the publisher, so a grain the measure does not publish is never offered, requested, and then silently reverted, while a measure declaring no grains keeps the full set because unknown is not none | Wrong metric, selection, coloring, count, an implicit no-data state, a suppressed or missing value coloured, sized, or formatted as zero, or a view level offered and accepted and then silently discarded so the screen appears to have lost the choice |
| WEB-003 | P0 | Component / `frontend` | History and source context | History is ordered and accessible; no-history and source/caveat/error context remain explicit | Inaccessible chart, hidden caveat, or ambiguous empty state |
| WEB-004 | P0 | Browser / `frontend` | Initial catalog/data/tile flow | Intercepted production UI requests load catalog and observations, decode a reviewed MVT, color the map, and show reconciled API distribution | Missing request, unhealthy tile, zero colored values, or legend mismatch |
| WEB-005 | P0 | Browser / `frontend` | Selection and keyboard flow | State/county selection pins the exact geography, loads history, and Enter/Escape selection controls work | Wrong geography/history or inaccessible keyboard interaction |
| WEB-006 | P0 | Browser / `frontend` | Partial, no-data, and failure states | ACS1 partial coverage, zero rows, and API 503 remain visible without stale observation counts | Silent fallback, stale data, or hidden failure state |
| WEB-007 | P0 | Static | Frontend dependency/build gate | `npm ci`, production audit, lint, `tsc --noEmit` strict typecheck, unit tests, and production Next.js build all exit zero with exact lockfile versions — and the build is the artifact the browser tier then serves, not one discarded after `check:bundle` and `check:csp` (WEB-068) | Audit, lint, type, test, or build failure |
| WEB-008 | P0 | CI | Frontend browser gate | Chromium installs and the browser suite runs from a fresh checkout, **against the production build the same job just made** (`next start`, not `next dev`; see WEB-068), with artifacts retained on failure | Missing runner, browser failure, or unavailable diagnostic artifact |
| WEB-009 | P0 | Unit / `frontend` | Versioned API client | Requests are constructed against `/api/v1` with empty parameters omitted and a cache mode that follows the kind of request -- `default` for a public read (a `GET` carrying neither a token nor a body), so the browser applies the `Cache-Control: public, max-age=<ttl>` the API answers with, and `no-store` for every token-bearing or writing request, which is somebody's own library or has nothing to reuse; a `/catalog/*` page already in flight is shared rather than fetched twice, by promise and never by stored answer, and never where a signal or a token makes sharing unsafe; limit/offset paging is deterministic and bounded; the `{"detail": ...}` error envelope decodes into a stable classified `ApiError` preserving `Retry-After`; abort signals pass through to the transport; and the request tracker suppresses stale async completions | A malformed request URL, an unbounded paging loop, a swallowed error detail, a lost cancellation, a stale response committed as current, a public answer refused the browser cache the API said it could hold, or a private answer left in a cache the next reader of that browser can reach |
| WEB-010 | P0 | Unit / `frontend` | Explorer URL state | Every supported explorer link parameter (`source`, `metric`, `state`, `geo`, `geo_level`, `map_mode`) parses to validated state, invalid values are dropped rather than propagated into requests, serialization omits defaults, and parse/serialize round-trips reproduce the same analysis request | A supported link breaking, an invalid parameter reaching a request, or two equivalent selections producing different URLs |
| WEB-011 | P0 | Unit / `frontend` | Capability-driven catalog source filter | Catalog source filter options derive from the API's published source list and degrade to the all-sources option when discovery is unavailable | A closed client-side source enumeration, or a catalog broken by unavailable discovery |
| WEB-012 | P0 | Component / `frontend` | Shared request-state pill | Every state in the shared request vocabulary maps to a deliberate visual class — only a completed healthy request renders as ok, failure-shaped states (bad/unauthorized/forbidden/rate-limited/unavailable/incompatible/conflict) render as errors, and everything else renders as caution — with the label and message visible | An unproven, stale, or failing state presented as healthy, or a state string with no deliberate mapping |
| WEB-013 | P0 | Unit + Browser / `frontend` | Capability-driven explorer sources | Explorer source membership derives from the `/catalog/capabilities` route declarations (the source-scoped latest + timeseries pair), parameter support comes from the declared route parameters, dataset facets derive from published metric identity, discovery failure degrades to the labeled offline fallback, and in the browser the tabs render from discovery, switching sources loads that source's catalog and observations, and `?source=` URLs reproduce the non-default source | A closed client-side source enumeration deciding membership, a tab for a source whose declared routes cannot answer the workflow, an explorer broken by unavailable discovery, or a source switch presenting another source's results |
| WEB-014 | P0 | Unit + Browser / `frontend` | Neutral-resource access for dispatch-shaped sources | A source joins the explorer through the access shapes its capability entry declares, preferring the neutral `/observations` resource wherever it is declared and falling back to the source-scoped latest/timeseries pair only for a source that publishes no neutral route — because the source-scoped pair reads the legacy cross-source union views, which key observations on that era's metric identity (`ACS:acs5:B01003_001`) rather than the glossary identity the catalog publishes (`CENSUS_ACS:acs5:B01003_001`), so a glossary code sent there matches nothing and answers an empty page indistinguishable from a geography with no published values — a segment-less source keeps its published `source_code` as its shareable key, every request filter comes from the declared `observation_filters`/route parameters so an undeclared one is neither sent nor silently dropped, `/distribution/bins` is requested only where declared, the neutral envelope's period bounds, unit, status, and dimensions map onto the explorer row shape without touching the published value, and a stratified answer is reported and left uncolored rather than collapsed to one row per geography | An undeclared filter reaching a request or a silently widened answer, a source read through a route whose metric identity the catalog does not publish, a dispatch-shaped source unreachable or reached by an invented route, a suppressed value rendered as a number, a published period range narrowed to one bound, or several declared-dimension series presented as one value per geography |
| WEB-015 | P0 | Unit + Browser / `frontend` | Catalog search, paging, and provenance | Catalog filters are exactly the ones `/catalog/metrics` declares and reach it unmodified (`active_only` dropped rather than sent as false), any filter change returns to the first page, limit/offset paging is deterministic against the API's published total with the displayed range anchored to the answered offset so a page in flight never relabels the loaded rows, an absent total is never replaced by a client-side count, every published provenance field renders in a fixed order while an unpublished one is omitted rather than shown as a placeholder, unpublished freshness reads as unknown rather than healthy, and catalog URLs reproduce the query, source, retired-metric, and page state | A client-side filter reporting a total the API never published, a page of results presented as the total, a stale page labelled with a new range, an invented provenance placeholder, an unknown freshness presented as healthy, or a catalog link that does not reopen its own results |
| WEB-016 | P0 | Unit + Browser / `frontend` | As-released exploration over published releases | The as-released surface is offered only where the capability entry declares `/observations/releases` and the neutral `scope`/`release` parameters; release identities come from `/observations/releases` rather than being inferred from a period or vintage; `scope=as_released` always answers on the neutral resource, carrying the source's declared neutral filters even for a source-scoped source; a pinned `release` travels only with `scope=as_released` and only where the resource declares it can be pinned; an unpinned as-released answer is reported as one series per release and left uncolored and unplotted rather than collapsed to whichever release sorted last; API-derived distribution bins are not requested for an as-released read because the route declares no scope; and the scope and pin round-trip through the shareable link and the export envelope | A release identity a client invented, a `release` sent without `scope=as_released` (a 422 by contract), an as-released answer collapsed to one value per geography, latest-publication bins presented as the released distribution, or a shared as-released link that does not reproduce its own analysis |

| WEB-020 | P0 | Unit + Browser / `frontend` | Aligned comparison presentations | The table, the scatter of the two published inputs, and the choropleth of one API-derived field are each offered only where the comparison can answer them — read from the verdict, the rows the response carried, the geographies plottable on both sides, the fields the response named as derived, and the vector layer's published geography fields — and every mode not offered names its published reason; a blocked pair presents no aligned view at all; the scatter plots each geography's own pair on independent axes (asserting no shared unit) and states how many geographies it could not plot instead of dropping them silently; the map colours only a field the response named as derived, leaves a geography missing either side uncoloured, and labels the legend as API-derived; and neither is ever the only way to read a value, with the full table always beside them | A presentation rendered for a comparison that cannot answer it, a blocked pair given an aligned view, a geography plotted or coloured at zero when one side published nothing, a published input coloured as though it were the comparison, or a chart or map that is the only path to a value |

the explorer and the comparison workspace save through one shared destination decision, which is the account whenever a token is held and the browser otherwise, with the destination stated on the control before the save and on the outcome after it, a browser-only save named as surviving nothing beyond this browser rather than reported as merely saved, and a refused account save reported rather than silently rewritten to the browser store | A configuration storing observation values, a token in a URL or shared link, user content in the address bar, a stale configuration presented as valid or silently rewritten, a version conflict resolved by overwriting, a 401 explained away, a local view silently dropped or coerced into a document the API would refuse, a save whose destination the user learns only afterwards, or a refused account save quietly redirected to the browser so the work appears safe somewhere the account cannot see |

A grain inside a state is chosen after one, the bound that keeps a 32k-place projection out of a `<select>`; a grain the projection publishes nothing for says exactly that; and a read still in flight says it is loading, because "none published" and "none arrived" are different statements.
